Let me think about the proof strategy.

We have STLC call-by-value with locally nameless, `if-then-else`, `Bool`. We need strong normalization.

The standard approach: unary logical relation / reducibility candidates. But CBV with strong normalization requires a bit of care: in CBV, `if t1 then t2 else t3` reduces t1 to a value first. A term `if t1 then t2 else t3` is SN if t1, t2, t3 are SN? Not quite: SN of the components doesn't imply SN of the whole in general? Actually for `if`, if t1 is SN, then any reduction path on t1 is finite; if t1 reduces to `true`, then it becomes t2 (SN); if to `false` becomes t3. But an infinite path on `if t1 t2 t3` would... well, the subterms t2/t3 are not reduced until t1 becomes a value. So if t1 SN and t2, t3 SN, then if t1 t2 t3 is SN. Yes.

Actually the standard "reducibility" for CBV: define the set of strongly normalizing terms, and then consider "SN values". The key lemma for application: if t1 is SN and t2 is SN, is t1 t2 SN? No! e.g. t1 = Omega? Omega isn't SN. Hmm, in CBV, `t1 t2 --> t1' t2` requires t1 step. If t1 SN and t2 SN, then app: any reduction path either reduces t1 (SN, so finite), then eventually t1 becomes a value and t2 reduces (SN, finite), and then if t1 is an abstraction, it opens. So we need: values of function type applied to values of argument type are SN.

The classical approach (as in Pierce's TAPL / Software Foundations `StlcSN` for CBV): use the logical relation defined as "hereditarily strongly normalizing" with the "SN value" notion. Actually the standard approach in SF: `norm t := strongly_normalizing t`, then define relation with `R_T t := SN t` at Bool and `R_{T1->T2} t := SN t /\ forall t2, R_{T1} t2 -> R_{T2} (t1 t2)` plus closure under expansion.

Actually the key trick for call-by-value is to define the logical relation on *values* first, then extend to arbitrary terms via "SN and reduces to a value satisfying the relation". Hmm. But simpler approach: define R_T(t) := SN(t) ∧ (for arrows) ∀ v, value v → R_T1(v) → R_T2 (t v)? Let's think.

Let me instead design the proof from scratch carefully.

Setting: CBV STLC. Goal: SN for well-typed closed terms.

Standard technique for CBV SN (I recall from Harper PFPL chapter 47 "Normalization" or from the "concrete syntax" chapter): Define the relation `t ∈ R_T` for closed terms (well, all terms) as:
- `R_Bool = { t | t is SN }`? Hmm, PFPL uses "t is a value and ..." approach:

PFPL's approach (Chapter 47? Actually Chapter 4? no): For CBV, PFPL defines expansion-based semantics: `t ∈ E_T` iff `t ⟼* v` for some `v ∈ V_T` where V are values. And uses "totality" (every well-typed term evaluates to a value) plus... For normalization (strong), they define "t is strongly normalizing" and show closure properties.

Alternatively, we can exploit a structural fact of STLC: no recursion, and the reduction of a term is "localized". Consider proving SN directly by induction on typing derivations, using a well-founded measure based on the *maximum reduction length* of subterms. Define `maxsteps t` = some measure? The problem: SN is defined inductively, and we can define the "height" of an SN derivation, but for a term not known SN we can't. However, we can bound the number of steps by a function of the typing derivation and the "sizes" of the term. E.g., define a function `size t` (number of nodes) and prove every step strictly decreases something like a "semantic size" µ_T(t) which for a well-typed term is a natural number. For CBV STLC, one can use the following measure: for each type T, define `||t||_T` = "hereditary size" and prove step decreases. That's essentially reducibility with a numeric measure, akin to "the number of steps is bounded".

Hmm, let me think about the standard inductive candidate approach again and work out the details, since it's cleanest.

I'll define, by induction on ty, a predicate on tm:

```
Fixpoint R (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => fun t => SN t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fun t => SN t /\ (forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.
```

Hmm wait, is R T1 u enough for u, or do we need u to be a value? Consider app lemma for t1 = tm_abs T1 body. We need R T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 body) u) given R T1 u. We have from R arrow: forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t1 u). So if t1 is in R(T1->T2), then app is in R T2 by definition. For case (c), we need to get from R T2 (app (abs body) v) to R T2 (open body v) where v is a value. This is "forward closure" at the step (app (abs body) v) --> open body v. So if we can prove forward closure lemma: `R T t -> t --> t' -> R T t'`, then case (c) is handled!

Forward closure for Bool: SN t and t --> t' gives SN t' by inversion on SN t. Easy.
Forward closure for Arrow: R T t = SN t ∧ ∀u, R T1 u -> R T2 (t u). Given t --> t', need SN t' (from SN t) and ∀u, R T1 u → R T2 (t' u). Given R T1 u, we have R T2 (t u). Need R T2 (t' u) from R T2 (t u) and t u --> t' u (ST_App1, needs locally_closed u — hmm! ST_App1 requires locally_closed t2!). 

Ah, subtle: ST_App1 requires `locally_closed t2`. So `t u --> t' u` requires locally_closed u. If u is not locally closed, the app doesn't step. But then t' u is also stuck? Not necessarily: it could be that t' u steps via ST_AppAbs if t' is an abs and u is a value... but if u is a value it is locally closed (values are abs with lc or true/false). Now let's carefully define the environment/substitution. Definitions:

```
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)
  end.
```

Option: define `V` by Fixpoint on ty where the arrow case is defined in terms of `R` at smaller types — and define R *inside* the Fixpoint as part of a pair. I.e.:

```
Fixpoint VR (T : ty) : (tm -> Prop) * (tm -> Prop) :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => (fun t => SN t /\ forall v, multi step t v -> value v -> (v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false),
                fun t => t = tm_true \/ t = tm_false)
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      let R1 := fst (VR T1) in
      let R2 := fst (VR T2) in
      let V1 := snd (VR T1) in
      let V2 := snd (VR T2) in
      ((fun t => SN t /\ forall v, multi step t v -> value v -> (exists T body, v = tm_abs T body /\ forall w, V1 w -> R2 (open body w))),
       (fun t => exists T body, t = tm_abs T body /\ forall w, V1 w -> R2 (open body w)))
  end.
Definition R T t := fst (VR T) t.
Definition V T t := snd (VR T) t.
```

Hmm, in the arrow case, R_{T1->T2} and V_{T1->T2} are defined using R1, R2, V1, V2 from smaller types. In the Bool case, R_Bool uses only SN and equality. Fixpoint VR T := match T with
 | Ty_Bool => (fun t => SN t /\ forall v, multi step t v -> value v -> VBool v, VBool)

```
Fixpoint VR (T : ty) : (tm -> Prop) * (tm -> Prop) :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool =>
      (fun t => strongly_normalizing t /\ (forall v, multi step t v -> value v -> (v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false)),
       fun t => t = tm_true \/ t = tm_false)
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      let '(R1, V1) := VR T1 in
      let '(R2, V2) := VR T2 in
      ((fun t => strongly_normalizing t /\
                 (forall v, multi step t v -> value v ->
                    exists T0 body, v = tm_abs T0 body /\ (forall w, V1 w -> R2 (open body w)))),
       (fun t => exists T0 body, t = tm_abs T0 body /\
                 (forall w, V1 w -> R2 (open body w))))
  end.
```

So R := lc ∧ SN ∧ reach. Let's define:

```
Definition R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\ (forall v, multi step t v -> value v -> V T v).
```

with V defined mutually? V T needs R at smaller types... and R T uses V T at the same type. So we still need the pair trick: VR T = (R_T, V_T) with V_T using R at smaller types and R_T using V_T at same type. Let's write:

```
Fixpoint VR (T : ty) : (tm -> Prop) * (tm -> Prop) :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool =>
      (fun t => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
                (forall v, multi step t v -> value v -> (v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false)),
       fun t => t = tm_true \/ t = tm_false)
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      let '(R1, V1) := VR T1 in
      let '(R2, _) := VR T2 in
      (fun t => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
                (forall v, multi step t v -> value v ->
                   exists T0 body, v = tm_abs T0 body /\ locally_closed (tm_abs T0 body) /\
                     (forall w, V1 w -> R2 (open body w))),
       fun t => exists T0 body, t = tm_abs T0 body /\ locally_closed (tm_abs T0 body) /\
                     (forall w, V1 w -> R2 (open body w)))
  end.
```

Note in the arrow case, the R component's condition is exactly V_{T1->T2} — duplicated. Good. The `let '(R1,V1) := VR T1 in ...` pattern: careful with dependent pattern matching in Fixpoint on ty where VR returns a pair; using `let '(a,b) := ... in` in a definition should be fine (it's a non-dependent match on a pair; should reduce).

Actually, defining with `let` patterns inside a Fixpoint: Coq might complain about guard condition? The recursive calls VR T1, VR T2 are on subterms, so fine.

Then:
```
Definition R T t := fst (VR T) t.
Definition V T t := snd (VR T) t.
```

Now, lemmas like R_sn require unfolding: `destruct T; simpl in *; tauto` etc. Since VR is a Fixpoint returning a pair, `simpl` on `R Ty_Bool t` should reduce: R Ty_Bool t = fst (VR Ty_Bool) t = (fun t => ...) t = .... With `Definition R T t := fst (VR T) t.` and `V T t := snd (VR T) t`, unfolding R and V and simplifying VR should work with `simpl`. But the `let '(R1,V1) := VR T1 in ...` inside may complicate reduction; `simpl` should handle it. Hmm, there might be issues with how `let` patterns reduce. Let me instead avoid `let` patterns in the return and use direct calls:

```
Fixpoint VR (T : ty) : (tm -> Prop) * (tm -> Prop) :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => (fun t => ... , fun t => ...)
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      (fun t => locally_closed t /\ SN t /\
                (forall v, multi step t v -> value v ->
                   exists T0 body, v = tm_abs T0 body /\ locally_closed (tm_abs T0 body) /\
                     (forall w, V T1 w -> R T2 (open body w))),
       fun t => exists T0 body, t = tm_abs T0 body /\ locally_closed (tm_abs T0 body) /\
                     (forall w, V T1 w -> R T2 (open body w)))
  end
with R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop := locally_closed t /\ SN t /\ ...
with V ...
```

Hmm, mutual Fixpoint with R and V calling each other at the same T — as discussed, the guard checker likely rejects V calling R at the same T? Wait: in this arrangement, the mutual definitions R and V... Let me think about how Coq's guard condition works with mutual fixpoints. From the Coq manual: "In the case of a mutual definition, the guard condition is that recursive calls must be made on a structurally smaller argument, with the notion of "same recursive call" extended..." Actually I recall that Coq's guard checker tracks, for each fixpoint in the block, a set of "principal arguments"; in a mutual block, a call from f to g is allowed if... I believe the rule is: for each function in the block, recursive calls within its body must be on structurally smaller arguments relative to that function's own principal argument. So R calling V at the same T fails, and V calling R at T1 ⊆ T is fine.

So the pair approach avoids this: a single function VR with one recursive argument T; all recursive calls are on T1/T2, strictly smaller. The pair is constructed once per T, so within the same T's component, R and V can refer to each other's *values* by destructuring the pair produced by the same recursive call... but we can't refer to "the pair currently being defined" inside the definition! Right: in defining VR (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) = (R_arrow, V_arrow), we want R_arrow to use V_arrow (same T). But V_arrow is defined in the same expression. We can write:

```
| Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
    let V_arrow := (fun t => exists ... (forall w, V T1 w -> R T2 (open body w))) in
    let R_arrow := (fun t => lc t /\ SN t /\ (forall v, multi ... -> value v -> V_arrow v)) in
    (R_arrow, V_arrow)
```

where V_arrow is a local let-binding and R_arrow uses it. This works! Since we duplicate the V definition inside the pair construction using a let. Let me write it as:

```
Fixpoint VR (T : ty) : (tm -> Prop) * (tm -> Prop) :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool =>
      let Vb := fun t : tm => t = tm_true \/ t = tm_false in
      (fun t => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
                (forall v, multi step t v -> value v -> Vb v), Vb)
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      let Va := fun t : tm => exists T0 body, t = tm_abs T0 body /\ locally_closed (tm_abs T0 body) /\
                                         (forall w, V T1 w -> R T2 (open body w)) in
      (fun t => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
                (forall v, multi step t v -> value v -> Va v), Va)
  end
with R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop := ...
```

Hmm, but that's back to mutual. Let me just use the let approach in a single fixpoint:

```
Fixpoint VR (T : ty) : (tm -> Prop) * (tm -> Prop) :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool =>
      let Vb : tm -> Prop := fun t => t = tm_true \/ t = tm_false in
      (fun t => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
                (forall v, multi step t v -> value v -> Vb v), Vb)
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      let Va : tm -> Prop :=
          fun t => exists T0 body, t = tm_abs T0 body /\ locally_closed (tm_abs T0 body) /\
                   (forall w, snd (VR T1) w -> fst (VR T2) (open body w)) in
      (fun t => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
                (forall v, multi step t v -> value v -> Va v), Va)
  end.
```

Here `snd (VR T1)` and `fst (VR T2)` are recursive calls on smaller types — fine for the guard condition. Then define `R T t := fst (VR T) t` and `V T t := snd (VR T) t`. But then when I unfold `V (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) w`, it becomes `snd (VR (Ty_Arrow T1 T2)) w` which simplifies to `Va w` = `exists ... forall w, snd (VR T1) w -> fst (VR T2) (open body w)`. And `snd (VR T1) w` is definitionally `V T1 w` (after unfolding V). So `simpl` should handle it if V is defined as `fun T t => snd (VR T) t`. Hmm, the unfolding: V T1 w = snd (VR T1) w by definition (delta). So in the simplified form we'd have snd(VR T1) w rather than V T1 w — same thing definitionally. Might make `simpl` loop? Since VR T1 is a Fixpoint applied to a constructor T1, it should reduce when T1 is a constructor. When T1 is a variable (in lemmas by induction/destruct on types), we'd destruct it eventually.

Hmm, what if we define V for arrow types not by recursion on the type but directly quoting R of the components:

That's the same. The mutual recursion R→V at same T is the issue. But note: R T t = lc t ∧ SN t ∧ ∀v reachable value, V T v. We could inline V T's definition into R T's definition (duplicating it), so R T never calls V T, only V at smaller types and R at smaller types. Then V T is still defined (needed as a hypothesis in the quantifiers), and V T calls R at smaller types. So both R and V at type T only depend on R/V at smaller types! Then a plain mutual Fixpoint with calls only on smaller types would pass the guard checker? Both R and V call R T1, V T1, R T2 (all smaller). And no same-type calls if we duplicate V inside R. So:

```
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => locally_closed t /\ SN t /\ (forall v, multi step t v -> value v -> (v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false))
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      locally_closed t /\ SN t /\
      (forall v, multi step t v -> value v ->
         exists T0 body, v = tm_abs T0 body /\ locally_closed (tm_abs T0 body) /\
                   (forall w, V T1 w -> R T2 (open body w)))
  end
with V (T : ty) (t : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => t = tm_true \/ t = tm_false
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      exists T0 body, t = tm_abs T0 body /\ locally_closed (tm_abs T0 body) /\
                (forall w, V T1 w -> R T2 (open body w))
  end.
```

Here R T calls V T1, R T2 (smaller than T = T1->T2, since T1 and T2 are direct subterms); V T calls V T1, R T2 — smaller. And in the Bool case no calls. Mutual fixpoint with calls on strict subterms: allowed. The duplicate V inside R for arrow is exactly the same formula as V's arrow case (good for lemma `R_to_V`). Note R_Bool's third component is exactly V_Bool.

Then lemma `R_V : R T t -> forall v, multi step t v -> value v -> V T v` proven by destruct T; simpl; tauto/intros; apply H. Since R arrow's condition is literally the same formula as V arrow, and R bool's is same as V bool, `destruct T; simpl in *; tauto` might work, or `intros; destruct T; simpl in *; intuition eauto`. Let's see: R Ty_Bool t = lc t ∧ SN t ∧ (∀v, multi... → Vb v), and V Ty_Bool v = Vb v. So after destruct T, simpl gives exactly matching. For arrow, R's third conjunct is literally V arrow's definition. So `tauto` should do it. But careful: `simpl` on `V (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) v` — since V is a Fixpoint on T, and T is a constructor, it reduces to the arrow case. Yes.

Great, this avoids the pair. But does the guard checker accept mutual Fixpoints where R's arrow case calls V T1 and R T2 (strict subterms) and V's arrow case calls V T1 and R T2? Yes: recursive calls on strict subterms are fine. And `struct T` annotations: for mutual fixpoints, the decreasing argument is specified per function. Should be fine.

**Part 0: Basic lemmas about the language.**

1. `value_no_step : forall v t, value v -> ~ (v --> t)`.
   Proof: intros v t Hv H. inversion Hv; subst; inversion H.
   Actually: destruct Hv: case v_abs: `inversion H` — H : tm_abs T t0 --> t; all step constructors' conclusions are tm_app... or tm_if..., so inversion should derive False? Does `inversion` on an impossible equality (tm_abs ... = tm_app ...) close the goal? `inversion H` where H's type has an inductive with no matching constructor: inversion will find that no constructor unifies and solve the goal? In Coq, `inversion H` on a hypothesis `step (tm_abs T t) t'` will examine the constructors of step; since unification fails for all, it solves the goal (like `inversion` on `S n = 0`). Yes, `inversion H` should solve it. But careful: ST_AppAbs's conclusion `tm_app (tm_abs T t) v --> open t v` doesn't unify with `tm_abs T0 t0 --> t'` since tm_app ≠ tm_abs. Similarly others. So inversion closes.
   
2. `value_lc : value v -> locally_closed v`. By destruct: v_abs gives lc; v_true/v_false: lc_true/lc_false (lc_at 0 tm_true).

3. `value_SN : value v -> strongly_normalizing v`. intros; apply SN_intro; intros t' Hstep; exfalso; eapply value_no_step; eauto.

4. `lc_step : forall t t', locally_closed t -> t --> t' -> locally_closed t'`. By induction on the step derivation.
   - ST_AppAbs: lc(app (abs T t) v) → lc(abs T t) ∧ lc v. Goal lc(open t v). Use `lc_open`: lc_at 1 t → lc v → lc (open t v). Since lc(abs T t) = lc_at 0 (tm_abs T t) = lc_at 1 t. Prove `lc_open : forall k t u, lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t)` by induction on t (generalizing k). Let's check each case:
     - bvar i: open_rec = if eqb k i then u else bvar i. Case eqb: lc_at k u. Case else: i ≠ k; from lc_at (S k) (bvar i): i < S k, i.e., i ≤ k; with i ≠ k → i < k → lc_at k (bvar i). Need `Nat.eqb k i = true → i = k` (Nat.eqb_eq); else i ≠ k (Nat.eqb_neq).
     - fvar x: lc_fvar.
     - app: lc_app + IHs.
     - abs T t1: goal lc_at k (tm_abs T (open_rec (S k) u t1)) = lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) u t1). Apply lc_abs; apply IH with k := S k; need lc_at (S (S k)) t1 (from lc_at (S k) (tm_abs T t1)) and lc_at (S k) u (from lc_at k u by monotonicity). 
     - if: lc_if + IHs.
   - ST_App1: lc(app t1 t2) → lc t1, lc t2; goal lc(app t1' t2): lc_app with IH on step for t1 (using lc t1).
   - ST_App2: similar.
   - ST_IfTrue: goal lc t1 (t1 is the then-branch); from lc(if true t1 t2) → lc t1.
   - ST_IfFalse: similar.
   - ST_If: goal lc(if t1' t2 t3): lc_if with IH and lc t2, lc t3.
   
   So `lc_step` by induction on step. Need to invert lc_at: for `lc_at k (tm_app a b)` → lc_at k a ∧ lc_at k b: `inversion H` gives it (the constructors of lc_at are disjoint by head symbol, so inversion works). Similarly for abs/if/true. Good.

5. `lc_mono : lc_at k t -> k <= k' -> lc_at k' t`. Induction on t. bvar: i < k ≤ k'. fvar, app, abs (need S k ≤ S k'), true/false, if. Easy with Nat lemmas.

No determinism needed! Great.

```
  destruct HR2 as (Hlc2 & Hsn2 & Hcond2).
  revert t2 Hlc2 Hcond2.
```

Wait: R is defined by Fixpoint on ty: R T1 t = match T1 with Ty_Bool => ... | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => ... end. If T1 is a variable, `destruct HR2 as (Hlc2 & Hsn2 & Hcond2)` wouldn't even work because R T1 t2 doesn't reduce to a conjunction syntactically; destruct would need to destruct T1 first.

Hmm. So the lemmas about R need to be proven by cases on the type. E.g., `R_sn : R T t -> SN t` proven by `destruct T; simpl; tauto`. But for lemmas where we both destruct and rebuild R at a *variable* type, we have a problem: we can't destruct R T t without knowing T's shape.

Solution: Don't destruct R at variable types; instead prove projection lemmas:
- `R_lc : R T t -> locally_closed t`
- `R_sn : R T t -> SN t`
- `R_cond : R T t -> forall v, multi step t v -> value v -> V T v`
each proven by `intros; destruct T; simpl in *; tauto` (for R_cond, the conclusion V T v also needs destruct T; `destruct T; simpl in *; tauto`).
And a constructor lemma:
- `R_intro : lc t -> SN t -> (forall v, multi step t v -> value v -> V T v) -> R T t`, proven by `destruct T; simpl; tauto`, or `intros; destruct T; simpl; auto`.

Then in proofs, we never destruct R at a variable type: we use projections. In the R_app proof, I should avoid destructing HR2. Instead use R_lc/R_sn/R_cond/R_intro. But then the induction hypotheses... Let's redo:

In R_app, the outer induction on SN t1 requires generalizing over the R-arrow hypothesis. But R-arrow hypothesis at variable type can be split via R_lc/R_sn/R_cond only into components — and to rebuild R for IH1's use at t1', we can use R_intro. So:

```
Lemma R_app : forall T1 T2 t1,
  R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t1 -> forall t2, R T1 t2 -> R T2 (tm_app t1 t2).
Proof.
  intros T1 T2 t1 HR1.
  (* generalize: prove by induction on SN t1, carrying the cond *)
  assert (Haux : forall (t1 : tm), SN t1 ->
     (forall v, multi step t1 v -> value v -> V (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) v) ->
     forall t2, R T1 t2 -> R T2 (tm_app t1 t2)).

  apply Haux with (t1 := t1); [apply R_sn in HR1 | apply R_cond in HR1]; auto.
```

Now, the fundamental lemma. Statement:

```
Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma,
    (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) ->
    (forall x U, Gamma x = Some U -> R U (sigma x)) ->
    R T (subst sigma t).
```

Wait, do we need the first condition (all sigma x lc)? We need lc of substituted terms: subst_lc requires ∀x, lc (sigma x) for all x (since a term can mention any fvar). But if we know all fvars in t are in Gamma... Hmm, alternatively, prove that the env condition implies lc for fvars in dom(Gamma) and separately prove that t's fvars ⊆ dom(Gamma). Now the final theorem:

```
Theorem normalization : forall t T, <{ empty |-- t \in T }> -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  pose proof (fundamental empty t T Hty (fun x => tm_fvar x) Hsigma_lc Henv) as HR.
  - forall x, lc (tm_fvar x): lc_fvar.
  - env: empty x = None, so the hypothesis `empty x = Some U` is absurd.
  Then R T t (since subst id t = t — need rewrite subst_id).
  Then R_sn gives SN t.
Qed.
```

lc_open_inv: `forall k t u, lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t) -> lc_at (S k) t`. Let's prove it by induction on t, generalizing k.
- bvar i: open_rec k u (bvar i) = if eqb k i then u else bvar i. Hyp: lc_at k (if ...). Destruct (Nat.eqb k i) eqn:E.

- fvar: lc_fvar.
- app: hyp lc_app → both; IH.
- abs T a: hyp lc_at k (abs T (open_rec (S k) u a)) → lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) u a). Need lc_at (S k) (abs T a) = lc_at (S (S k)) a. Apply IH (k := S k) with u: need lc_at (S k) u (from lc_at k u by monotone) and lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) u a) (given). ✓
- true/false/if: trivial (lc_true etc. / lc_if IH).

Good. Need lc_mono : lc_at k t -> forall k', k <= k' -> lc_at k' t. Prove by induction on t (generalizing k). Actually easier: prove `lc_at_mono : forall k k' t, k <= k' -> lc_at k t -> lc_at k' t` by induction on t.
- bvar i: i < k <= k'.
- abs: k <= k' → S k <= S k' (Nat.succ_le_mono); apply lc_abs, IH.
- etc.

```
Lemma subst_open_rec : forall sigma k u t,
  subst sigma (open_rec k u t) = open_rec k (subst sigma u) (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  intros sigma k u t; revert k; induction t; intros k; simpl.
  - (* bvar i *) destruct (Nat.eqb k i); reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - simpl; rewrite IHt1, IHt2; reflexivity.
  - simpl; rewrite IHt; reflexivity.  (* careful: IHt is forall k, need k := S k *)
  ...
```
For abs: LHS = subst sigma (tm_abs T (open_rec (S k) u t)) = tm_abs T (subst sigma (open_rec (S k) u t)) = tm_abs T (open_rec (S k) (subst sigma u) (subst sigma t)) by IH with k := S k. RHS = open_rec k (subst sigma u) (tm_abs T (subst sigma t)) = tm_abs T (open_rec (S k) (subst sigma u) (subst sigma t)). ✓.

Now let's also handle the R_app_aux lc part and the general shape. Let me write the aux lemma statement concretely:

```
Lemma R_app_aux : forall T1 T2 (t1 : tm),
  locally_closed t1 ->
  (forall v, multi step t1 v -> value v -> V (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) v) ->
  strongly_normalizing t1 ->
  forall (t2 : tm),
  locally_closed t2 ->
  (forall v, multi step t2 v -> value v -> V T1 v) ->
  strongly_normalizing t2 ->
  R T2 (tm_app t1 t2).
```

Proof:
```
  intros T1 T2 t1 Hlc1 Hcond1 Hsn1.
  revert Hlc1 Hcond1.
  induction Hsn1 as [t1 Hstep1 IH1].
  intros Hlc1 Hcond1 t2 Hlc2 Hcond2 Hsn2.
  revert Hlc2 Hcond2.
  induction Hsn2 as [t2 Hstep2 IH2].
  intros Hlc2 Hcond2.
```
Hmm wait, after `revert Hlc1 Hcond1; induction Hsn1`, the motive is P t1 := lc t1 -> cond t1 -> forall t2, lc t2 -> cond t2 -> SN t2 -> R T2 (app t1 t2). The `induction Hsn1` will abstract t1 in the goal, but does the goal still mention Hsn1? No, Hsn1 is the hypothesis being inducted. Good.

But hmm, the order of quantifiers in the motive: after revert, the goal is `locally_closed t1 -> (forall v, multi step t1 v -> value v -> V (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) v) -> forall t2, locally_closed t2 -> (forall v, ...) -> strongly_normalizing t2 -> R T2 (tm_app t1 t2)`. Then induction on Hsn1 gives P t1. Then `intros Hlc1 Hcond1 t2 Hlc2 Hcond2 Hsn2` — the order: Hlc1, Hcond1, t2, Hlc2, Hcond2, Hsn2. ✓ Then `revert Hlc2 Hcond2` gives goal `lc t2 -> cond t2 -> R T2 (app t1 t2)` and then `induction Hsn2` abstracts t2 (Hsn2 in context, no other t2-dependent hyps — Hstep2/IH2 don't exist yet). Then the case goal: `lc t2 -> cond t2 -> R T2 (app t1 t2)` with Hstep2, IH2. Then intros Hlc2 Hcond2.

Then:
```
  assert (Hsucc : forall s, tm_app t1 t2 --> s -> R T2 s).
  { intros s Hst. inversion Hst; subst.
    - (* ST_AppAbs *) 
    - (* ST_App1 *) 
    - (* ST_App2 *) }
```
Hmm, the inversion of `step (tm_app t1 t2) s` — the constructors ST_AppAbs, ST_App1, ST_App2 have conclusions with tm_app head, so inversion gives 3 cases (ST_AppAbs gives t1 = tm_abs T t, t2 = v; ST_App1 gives t1 --> t1' and lc t2 and s = tm_app t1' t2; ST_App2 gives value t1 and t2 --> t2' and s = tm_app t1 t2'). But careful: ST_App1's premise `t1 --> t1'` — after inversion, we get t1 possibly substituted. Let me handle each case carefully in Coq; I'll experiment.

Then:
```
  apply R_intro.
  - apply lc_app; assumption.
  - apply SN_intro; intros s Hs; apply R_sn; apply Hsucc; assumption.
  - intros v Hm Hval. destruct Hm as [| s v' Hs Hm'].
    + inversion Hval.  (* v = tm_app t1 t2 not a value *)
    + apply (R_cond (Hsucc s Hs)); assumption.
```
Wait, destruct Hm: multi_refl case has v = tm_app t1 t2 (the index). Actually `destruct Hm` on `multi step (tm_app t1 t2) v`: multi_refl : forall x, multi R x x — so the case is when v = (tm_app t1 t2). After destruct, the goal becomes `V T2 (tm_app t1 t2)` with Hval : value (tm_app t1 t2) → contradiction by inversion. Hmm, destruct will substitute v := tm_app t1 t2 in the goal and Hval. Then `inversion Hval` should close (since value has no constructor for app). Let's check: value constructors: v_abs (conclusion value (tm_abs ...)), v_true, v_false. None unify with value (tm_app a b). So inversion closes. But careful: `destruct Hm` might also substitute in Hval, yielding `value (tm_app t1 t2)`; then `inversion Hval` closes the goal. ✓

For the multi_step case: Hm' : multi step s v; Hs : step (tm_app t1 t2) s. Goal V T2 v. `apply (R_cond (Hsucc s Hs) v Hm' Hval)` — R_cond : R T2 s -> forall v, multi step s v -> value v -> V T2 v. ✓

Then R_intro requires proving `R T2 (tm_app t1 t2)` = lc ∧ SN ∧ cond. Apply R_intro with the three parts. ✓ (R_intro : lc t -> SN t -> cond t -> R T t.)

Now, the ST_App1 case of Hsucc: we have Hst1 : t1 --> t1' (some name), Hlc2' : locally_closed t2 (from the constructor), and goal R T2 (tm_app t1' t2)? Wait, after inversion and subst, the goal is `R T2 (tm_app t1' t2)`? The constructor is `ST_App1 : forall t1 t1' t2, t1 --> t1' -> locally_closed t2 -> tm_app t1 t2 --> tm_app t1' t2`. So inversion gives Hstep : t1 --> t1', Hlc : lc t2, and s = tm_app t1' t2. Goal R T2 (tm_app t1' t2). Apply IH1 with Hstep: IH1 : ∀t1', step t1 t1' → lc t1' -> cond t1' -> forall t2, lc t2 -> cond t2 -> SN t2 -> R T2 (app t1' t2). Need:
- lc t1' : lc_step Hlc1 Hstep.
- cond t1' : ∀v, multi step t1' v → value v → V (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) v. From Hcond1: given multi t1' v, then multi t1 v by multi_step t1 t1' v Hstep H. ✓
- t2, lc t2 (from inversion), cond t2 (Hcond2), SN t2 (= SN_intro t2 Hstep2). ✓
✓

ST_App2 case: constructor `ST_App2 : forall v1 t2 t2', value v1 -> t2 --> t2' -> tm_app v1 t2 --> tm_app v1 t2'`. Inversion of step (tm_app t1 t2) s: t1 = v1, s = tm_app v1 t2'... hmm, after subst t1 := v1 (so Hlc1 : lc v1, Hcond1 adapted). Goal R T2 (tm_app t1 t2')? Since s = tm_app v1 t2' and t1 = v1, goal is R T2 (tm_app v1 t2') = R T2 (tm_app t1 t2'). Apply IH2 with Hstep : t2 --> t2': IH2 : ∀t2', step t2 t2' → lc t2' -> cond t2' -> R T2 (app t1 t2'). Need lc t2' (lc_step Hlc2 Hstep) and cond t2' (from Hcond2 and the step). ✓

Great.

Now let's think about R_intro/R_lc/R_sn/R_cond proofs.

Definitions:

```
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
               (forall v, multi step t v -> value v -> (v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false))
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall v, multi step t v -> value v ->
         exists T0 body, v = tm_abs T0 body /\ locally_closed (tm_abs T0 body) /\
           (forall w, V T1 w -> R T2 (open body w)))
  end
with V (T : ty) (t : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => t = tm_true \/ t = tm_false
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      exists T0 body, t = tm_abs T0 body /\ locally_closed (tm_abs T0 body) /\
        (forall w, V T1 w -> R T2 (open body w))
  end.
```

Hmm, mutual Fixpoints in Prop with this shape: Coq might complain "Cannot guess decreasing argument"? We specify `{struct T}` for both. Let me test.

Potential issue: the mutual fixpoint's guard condition: in R's arrow branch, `V T1 w` is a call to V with T1, which is a subterm of T = Ty_Arrow T1 T2 ✓ and `R T2 (open body w)` with T2 subterm ✓. In V's arrow branch, `V T1` ✓ and `R T2` ✓. Fine.

But hmm, does Coq accept mutual fixpoints in Prop with a `struct` annotation for each? Yes: `Fixpoint f (n:nat) {struct n} : Prop := ... with g (n:nat) {struct n} : Prop := ...`. Should be OK.

Then:
```
Lemma R_lc : forall T t, R T t -> locally_closed t.
Proof. intros T; destruct T; simpl; intros t H; tauto. Qed.
```
Hmm, `destruct T; simpl` — after destruct T, the goal is `forall t, R Ty_Bool t -> lc t`; `simpl` unfolds R Ty_Bool to the conjunction; `intros t H; tauto` or `intros; apply H`? H : lc t /\ SN t /\ cond. `tauto` should extract. Use `intuition`/`tauto`.

`R_sn`, `R_cond`, `R_intro` similar.

For R_cond: goal `forall T t, R T t -> forall v, multi step t v -> value v -> V T v`. destruct T; simpl; intros; tauto? For Bool: R's third conjunct is `∀v, multi... → value v → (v = true ∨ v = false)` and V Ty_Bool v = (v = true ∨ v = false). So H's third conjunct applied to v and the multis gives V. `simpl in *; tauto` might handle. For arrow: R's third conjunct is literally the exists formula and V arrow is the same formula. So `tauto` should work if simplification brings them to the same form. Let me be careful: R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t simplifies to `lc t /\ SN t /\ (∀v, multi t v → value v → ∃T0 body, ...)` and V (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) v simplifies to `∃T0 body, v = tm_abs ...`. In the goal `V (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) v` where v is a variable, `simpl` gives the exists formula. Applying H's third conjunct to v, Hmulti, Hval gives exactly that term. `tauto` might work but the `exists` matching... Since the terms are syntactically identical after simplification, `tauto` should succeed. If not, `intuition eauto` or manual.

Hmm, one concern: the recursive calls `V T1 w` and `R T2 (open body w)` remain as `V T1 w` / `R T2 ...` after simplification? Since T1, T2 are variables, the Fixpoint doesn't reduce. So `simpl` leaves them as `V T1 w`. Good, so the two formulas are syntactically identical provided both simplify the same way. But careful: when we destruct T = Ty_Arrow T1 T2, then `R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t` simplifies. But inside the simplified formula there's `V T1 w` where V is the mutual Fixpoint — fine.

But in the lemma statements, we might write `V T v` with T a variable; `destruct T` handles it.

I think this works. Alternatively, to be safe, I can state R_intro/R_cond etc. with explicit `intros; destruct T; simpl in *; tauto` and adjust.

Now let me think about proving `V_R : V T v -> R T v` — destruct T; simpl; intros.

Bool case: HV : v = true ∨ v = false. Need lc v ∧ SN v ∧ (∀v', multi step v v' → value v' → v' = true ∨ v' = false). 
- lc: destruct HV; subst; constructor (lc_true/lc_false).
- SN: destruct HV; subst; apply value_SN; constructor.
- cond: intros v' Hm Hval; apply multi_value_eq in Hm... define lemma `multi_of_value : forall v v', value v -> multi step v v' -> v' = v`. Then subst; exact HV.
Arrow case: HV : ∃T0 body, v = abs T0 body ∧ lc(abs T0 body) ∧ quant. Need lc v (from Heq + lc), SN v (value_SN with v_abs lc), cond: ∀v', multi v v' → value v' → V_arrow v'. Using multi_of_value: v' = v; then need V_arrow v — reconstruct from HV: exists T0, body... ✓. Actually after subst v' = v, the goal V (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) v is exactly HV (after unfolding) — so `exact HV` or `assumption`.

Now `multi_of_value`:
```
Lemma multi_of_value : forall v v', value v -> multi step v v' -> v' = v.
Proof.
  intros v v' Hval Hm. destruct Hm as [| s z Hs Hm].
  - reflexivity.
  - exfalso. exact (value_no_step v s Hval Hs).
Qed.
```
Check the destruct: multi_refl x : multi R x x, so the first case has v' = v (both are the same x); goal v = v → reflexivity. multi_step x y z : R x y -> multi R y z -> multi R x z; here x = v, z = v', so Hs : step v s, Hm : multi step s v'. Then value_no_step gives False. ✓. But careful: after destruct, in the second case the goal is v' = v; exfalso works.

Now `V_lc : V T v -> locally_closed v`:
- Bool: HV : v = true ∨ v = false → destruct; subst; constructor.
- Arrow: HV : ∃T0 body, v = abs T0 body ∧ lc(abs T0 body) ∧ ...; destruct; subst; assumption.
And `V_value : V T v -> value v`:
- Bool: true/false → v_true/v_false.
- Arrow: v = abs T0 body, lc → v_abs lc.

Now, the fundamental lemma. Let me write out the plan in Coq-ish pseudocode.

```
(** Substitution *)
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm := ...

Lemma subst_open_rec : ...
Lemma subst_id : forall t, subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t.

(** Fresh atoms *)
Fixpoint max_atom (l : list atom) : atom := ...
Lemma In_le_max : ...
Lemma fresh_notin : forall l, ~ In (max_atom l + 1) l.  (* or S *)

(** fvars *)
Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom := ...

Lemma subst_ext_fresh : forall sigma1 sigma2 t x,
  ~ In x (fvars t) ->
  (forall y, y <> x -> sigma1 y = sigma2 y) ->
  subst sigma1 t = subst sigma2 t.
```
Hmm, alternatively formulate with the update-substitution function:
```
Definition subst_update (sigma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (u : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y.
```
Then `subst_ext_fresh` with sigma1 = subst_update sigma x w and sigma2 = sigma: for y ≠ x, subst_update sigma x w y = sigma y (since eqb x y = false when x ≠ y). Need Nat.eqb_neq.

Proof of subst_ext_fresh by induction on t:
- bvar: both give bvar i. ✓
- fvar y: In y (fvars (fvar y)) = In y (y::nil) → y ≠ x. Then sigma1 y = sigma2 y. ✓
- app/abs/if: IHs; for abs, fvars (abs T a) = fvars a. ✓
- true/false. ✓

Then the T_Abs case: subst (subst_update sigma x w) t1 = subst sigma t1 using subst_ext_fresh with x not in fvars t1.

And `subst_update_eq : subst_update sigma x w x = w` (eqb_refl). And `subst_update_neq`.

Also the commutation: subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x)) = open (subst sigma' t1) (subst sigma' (fvar x)) = open (subst sigma' t1) (sigma' x) = open (subst sigma' t1) w.

Now the environment condition. Let me define:
```
Definition env_ok (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> R T (sigma x).
```

Fundamental lemma:
```
Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma, (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) ->
  env_ok Gamma sigma -> R T (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty.
  induction Hty as [Gamma x T Heq | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody IHbody | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 | Gamma | Gamma | Gamma t1 t2 t3 T H1 IH1 H2 IH2 H3 IH3]; ...
```
Hmm, the T_Abs case: Coq's `induction Hty` will handle the premise `forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ...` — the IH should be `forall x, ~ In x L -> forall sigma, ...`. But careful: the motive includes `forall sigma, lc_sigma -> env_ok -> R T (subst sigma t)`; for the T_Abs premise, Coq generalizes: the IH for a premise of the form `forall x, _ -> has_type ...` becomes `forall x, _ -> (motive applied to that judgment)`. I believe this works.

Let me write the proof cases:

1. T_Var: Gamma x = Some T. Goal R T (subst sigma (tm_fvar x)) = R T (sigma x). Apply Henv; exact Heq.

2. T_Abs: Goal R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) (subst sigma (tm_abs T1 t1)) = R (T1->T2) (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1)).
   Apply R_intro.
   - lc: `lc_abs` requires lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1). 
     Prove `Hlcb : lc_at 1 t1`: how? Option A: apply has_type_lc to the original typing judgment. We need the original judgment. Use `assert (Hty0 : has_type Gamma (tm_abs T1 t1) (T1->T2))`? In the induction, we have the constructor info. Hmm, after `induction Hty`, the T_Abs case has context: L, Gamma, T1, T2, t1, Hbody, IHbody, and the goal. Hty itself is consumed. To get the whole judgment, we can prove has_type_lc with a separate induction (not using Hty), and then in this case reconstruct: has_type Gamma (tm_abs T1 t1) (T1->T2) = T_Abs L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody. So we can apply has_type_lc to `T_Abs L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody`. Alternatively, prove has_type_lc inside this case using IHbody: from IHbody x Hx (for fresh x ∉ L) with... hmm, IHbody gives `forall sigma, lc_sigma -> env_ok -> R T2 (subst sigma (open t1 (fvar x)))`; from R_lc we get lc (subst sigma (open t1 (fvar x))) — then we'd need to go from lc of the substituted term to lc_at 1 t1 — harder.
     
     Simplest: prove has_type_lc separately by its own induction, and in the T_Abs case of fundamental, apply it to the reconstructed judgment `T_Abs L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody` — wait, can we refer to the constructor with Hbody? Yes: `has_type_lc _ _ _ (T_Abs L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody)`. Since the constructor is available. But hold on: in the induction, Coq names the constructor arguments; `Hbody` is the hypothesis `forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2`. Then `T_Abs L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody : has_type Gamma (tm_abs T1 t1) (T1->T2)`. So has_type_lc gives lc_at 0 (tm_abs T1 t1) = lc_at 1 t1 (definitionally? lc_at 0 (tm_abs T1 t1) is an inductive proposition; `lc_at 0 (tm_abs T1 t1)` reduces to something whose proof must be lc_abs ... — but as a *proposition*, lc_at 0 (tm_abs T1 t1) is not definitionally equal to lc_at 1 t1; we need inversion: `inversion H as [| | | k T t H | | |]; subst` — simpler: prove a lemma `lc_abs_inv : lc_at k (tm_abs T t) -> lc_at (S k) t` by `intros H; inversion H; assumption` (inversion on lc_at with abs head: only lc_abs matches; gives H1 : lc_at (S k) t... wait lc_abs constructor: lc_abs : forall k T t1, lc_at (S k) t1 -> lc_at k (tm_abs T t1). Inversion of lc_at k0 (tm_abs T t) gives k0 = k, T, t and hypothesis lc_at (S k) t. ✓).
     
     Alternatively — maybe simpler: define has_type_lc to directly give the needed form? It's fine.
     
     So Hlcb : lc_at 1 t1 from has_type_lc + lc_abs_inv. Then subst_lc_at 1 t1 sigma Hlcb (∀x, lc_at 1 (sigma x)) — the latter from Hlc_sigma and lc_mono (k=0 ≤ 1). Then lc_abs gives lc (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1)).
   - SN: value_SN with value (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1)) via v_abs Hlcabs.
   - cond: as described.
   
   For the cond part: need to prove ∀v, multi step (abs) v → value v → V_arrow v. Intros v Hm Hval. destruct Hm:
   + multi_refl: v = abs T1 (subst sigma t1). Need V (T1->T2) (abs T1 (subst sigma t1)). Unfold V (simpl). Provide exists T1, (subst sigma t1), eq_refl, Hlcabs, and ∀w, V T1 w → R T2 (open (subst sigma t1) w).
     For each w with HVw : V T1 w:
       Choose x fresh from L ++ fvars t1.
       Have HxL : ~ In x L. Have Hxfv : ~ In x (fvars t1).
       Let sigma' := subst_update sigma x w.
       Have Hbodyx := Hbody x HxL : has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2.
       Have IHx := IHbody x HxL sigma' ... : R T2 (subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x))).
         Need: (a) ∀y, lc (sigma' y): destruct (eqb x y): if true then y = x → sigma' y = w → V_lc HVw. If false → sigma y → Hlc_sigma y.
         (b) env_ok (update Gamma x T1) sigma': for y, U with update Gamma x T1 y = Some U. destruct (eqb x y) eqn:E.
             - true: y = x, so update = Some T1, so U = T1. Need R T1 (sigma' y) = R T1 w: from V_R HVw. (Need to reconcile U = T1 and sigma' y = w.)
             - false: update Gamma x T1 y = Gamma y = Some U; apply Henv.
       Then rewrite: subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x)) = open_rec 0 (subst sigma' (fvar x)) (subst sigma' t1) = open (subst sigma' t1) w. Use subst_open_rec (k=0, u := fvar x) and simplify subst sigma' (fvar x) = sigma' x = w.
       Then rewrite subst sigma' t1 = subst sigma t1 via subst_ext_fresh with x ∉ fvars t1: for y ≠ x, sigma' y = sigma y — need eqb x y = false from y ≠ x: Nat.eqb_neq. Careful about direction: subst_update sigma x w y = if Nat.eqb x y then w else sigma y. Given y ≠ x, Nat.eqb x y = false. ✓
       Conclude.
   + multi_step s Hstep: value (abs ...) and step (abs ...) s → contradiction: value_no_step. exfalso; eapply value_no_step; eauto.

3. T_App: goal R T2 (tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)). Apply R_app (or directly R_app_aux with projections). IH1 : R (T1->T2) (subst sigma t1), IH2 : R T1 (subst sigma t2). Apply R_app with those.

4. T_True: goal R Ty_Bool tm_true.
   R_intro: lc_true; value_SN v_true; cond: intros v Hm Hval; destruct Hm: refl → v = tm_true → left; eq_refl. step → absurd (value_no_step v_true).
5. T_False similar.
6. T_If: R_if.

Now R_app's statement: `R_app : forall T1 T2 t1, R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t1 -> forall t2, R T1 t2 -> R T2 (tm_app t1 t2)`. Proof: intros; apply R_app_aux with (R_lc HR1) (R_cond HR1) (R_sn HR1) (R_lc HR2) (R_cond HR2) (R_sn HR2). ✓

And R_if:
```
Lemma R_if : forall T t1 t2 t3, R Ty_Bool t1 -> R T t2 -> R T t3 -> R T (tm_if t1 t2 t3).
```
Proof: intros T t1 t2 t3 HR1 HR2 HR3. 
Get Hlc1 := R_lc HR1, Hsn1 := R_sn HR1, Hcond1 := R_cond HR1.
revert Hlc1 Hcond1. induction Hsn1 as [t1 Hstep1 IH1]. intros Hlc1 Hcond1.

- lc: lc_if Hlc1 (R_lc HR2) (R_lc HR3).
- Hsucc : forall s, tm_if t1 t2 t3 --> s -> R T s. intros s Hs; inversion Hs; subst.

  * ST_IfFalse: t1 = tm_false, s = t3 → HR3.
  * ST_If: t1 --> t1', s = tm_if t1' t2 t3. Apply IH1 with Hstep: needs lc t1', cond t1', (t2, t3 and HR2, HR3 are fixed). IH1 : ∀t1', step t1 t1' → lc t1' -> cond_bool t1' -> R T (if t1' t2 t3)? IH1 : ∀t1', step t1 t1' → P t1'. ✓
- SN: SN_intro with Hsucc.
- cond: like the app case: destruct multi; refl → v = if... value → inversion; step → R_cond (Hsucc s Hs).
✓

Hmm, but in the R_if induction, is `Hcond1`'s type `forall v, multi step t1 v -> value v -> V Ty_Bool v`? Yes via R_cond. Fine.

```coq
(** ** Auxiliary definitions and lemmas for the normalization proof *)

(* Substitution of free variables *)
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)
  end.

Definition subst_update (sigma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (u : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y.

Lemma subst_update_eq : forall sigma x u, subst_update sigma x u x = u.
Proof. intros; unfold subst_update; rewrite Nat.eqb_refl; reflexivity. Qed.

Lemma subst_update_neq : forall sigma x u y, y <> x -> subst_update sigma x u y = sigma y.
Proof. intros; unfold subst_update; apply Nat.eqb_neq in H; rewrite H; reflexivity. Qed.
```
Wait, Nat.eqb_neq : forall x y, x <> y <-> eqb x y = false? Let's check: In PeanoNat, `Nat.eqb_neq : forall x y, x <> y <-> (x =? y) = false`. The order is (x =? y). Our goal has Nat.eqb x y with y <> x — need x ≠ y (symmetric). So: `apply Nat.eqb_neq; auto` with x ≠ y derived by congruence. Let me write `intros; unfold subst_update; destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E; [apply Nat.eqb_eq in E; subst; contradiction | reflexivity]`. Simpler: use Nat.eqb_eq/Nat.eqb_neq.

Actually, careful with the direction of eqb: `Nat.eqb x y`. We need false given y ≠ x. Nat.eqb_eq : (x =? y) = true <-> x = y. So if eqb = true then x = y, contradicting y ≠ x. Good.

```
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: rest => Nat.max x (max_atom rest)

  - inversion H.
```

```
Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app a b => fvars a ++ fvars b
```

Now lc_step:
```
Lemma lc_step : forall t t', locally_closed t -> t --> t' -> locally_closed t'.
Proof.
  intros t t' Hlc Hstep. induction Hstep.
  - (* ST_AppAbs *) simpl in Hlc. apply lc_app_inv in Hlc as [Hlab Hlv]. apply lc_abs_inv in Hlab. apply lc_open; auto.
  - (* ST_App1 *) simpl in Hlc. apply lc_app_inv in Hlc as [Hl1 Hl2]. apply lc_app; auto.
  - (* ST_App2 *) simpl in Hlc. apply lc_app_inv in Hlc as [Hl1 Hl2]. apply lc_app; auto.
  - (* ST_IfTrue *) simpl in Hlc. apply lc_if_inv in Hlc as [H1 [H2 H3]]. assumption.
  - (* ST_IfFalse *) ... assumption.
  - (* ST_If *) simpl in Hlc. apply lc_if_inv in Hlc as [H1 [H2 H3]]. apply lc_if; auto.
Qed.
```
Note: `induction Hstep` — the induction principle for step: careful, the step relation is indexed by two terms; induction on a proof of step t t' with local hypotheses Hlc. Since the IH for recursive premises (ST_App1 etc.) will be `locally_closed t1 -> locally_closed t1'`? Hmm, `induction Hstep` gives IHs for recursive step premises: e.g. ST_App1's premise `t1 --> t1'` gets IH : locally_closed t1 -> locally_closed t1'. But our hypothesis Hlc : locally_closed (tm_app t1 t2) is fixed in the context and mentions the original terms. Hmm, when doing induction on Hstep, Coq abstracts the indices t, t'; the local hypothesis Hlc : locally_closed t mentions t — this will cause an error: "Abstracting over t leads to a term ... depending on it" — actually Coq may auto-revert Hlc? No: if Hlc depends on t and we do induction on Hstep : step t t', Coq will complain that it cannot generalize. Standard fix: revert Hlc first, then induction, then reintroduce. But then the IH is `locally_closed t1 -> locally_closed t1'`, and in each case after `intro Hlc`, we can use the IH by first deriving the needed subterm lc. Let's do:

```
Lemma lc_step : forall t t', t --> t' -> locally_closed t -> locally_closed t'.
Proof.
  intros t t' Hstep. induction Hstep; intros Hlc.
  ...
```
Ordering Hlc after `induction Hstep` via `intros Hlc` in each case — but the goal's form must match: goal `locally_closed t'`; after `intros Hlc` we have Hlc : locally_closed t. Then per case.

Case ST_AppAbs: t = tm_app (tm_abs T t) v, t' = open t v. Hlc : locally_closed (tm_app (tm_abs T t) v). We have Hlab : locally_closed (tm_abs T t) from lc_app_inv, plus premise `locally_closed (tm_abs T t)` given by the constructor. Either way.
Goal locally_closed (open t v). Use lc_open: lc_at (S 0) t (from lc_abs_inv Hlab) and lc v (from lc_app_inv Hlc). 
Note `open t v = open_rec 0 v t`.

Let me define lc_open as:
```
Lemma lc_open : forall t u, lc_at 1 t -> locally_closed u -> locally_closed (open t u).
Proof. intros; apply lc_open_rec with (k := 0); auto. Qed.
```
where lc_open_rec : forall k t u, lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t). Let's prove that (as discussed).

Case ST_App1: Hlc : lc (tm_app t1 t2); Hstep premise t1 --> t1'; IH : locally_closed t1 -> locally_closed t1'. Goal lc (tm_app t1' t2). lc_app with IH Hl1 and Hl2.
Case ST_App2: goal lc (tm_app v1 t2'); Hlc gives lc v1, lc t2; IH : lc t2 -> lc t2'. Apply.
Case ST_IfTrue: goal lc t1 (the then-branch); from Hlc : lc (if true t1 t2) via lc_if_inv.
Case ST_IfFalse similar.
Case ST_If: goal lc (if t1' t2 t3); Hlc gives lc t1, lc t2, lc t3; IH: lc t1 -> lc t1'.

Good.

One more consideration: with R defined as a mutual Fixpoint, `destruct T; simpl` in lemma proofs should work. But there's a catch: when we have `R T t` with T a variable and we do `destruct T`, Coq substitutes T = Ty_Bool or T = Ty_Arrow T1 T2, and `simpl` should reduce R (Ty_Bool) t. Since R is a Fixpoint with `struct T`, it will ι-reduce. ✓

Another catch: `V T v` where T is a variable in the cond part of R's definition: after destruct T, they become the same formula. ✓

```coq
(** ** Substitution of free variables *)

Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)
  end.

Definition subst_update (sigma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (u : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y.

Lemma subst_update_eq : forall sigma x u, subst_update sigma x u x = u.
Proof. intros. unfold subst_update. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity. Qed.

Lemma subst_update_neq : forall sigma x u y, y <> x -> subst_update sigma x u y = sigma y.
Proof.
  intros. unfold subst_update.
  destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
  - apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst. contradiction.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.
```
Hmm, `apply Nat.eqb_eq in E` gives x = y, then `subst` would substitute x := y... then contradiction with H : y <> x → after subst, y <> y. `contradiction` works? H : y <> y maybe becomes H : y <> x with x replaced... Let me instead: `apply Nat.eqb_eq in E; subst; apply H; reflexivity.` Hmm, careful with which variable gets substituted. Let me write `exfalso; apply H; symmetry; assumption` — hmm. Simpler: 
```
  destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
  - apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. congruence.
  - reflexivity.
```
`congruence` with H : y <> x and E : x = y should close. ✓

Or use `Nat.eqb_neq`: `apply Nat.eqb_neq in E`? Nat.eqb_neq : forall x y : nat, x <> y <-> (x =? y) = false. With E : (x =? y) = true, we can't directly apply. Use EqDec. I'll go with congruence.

```
Fixpoint max_atom (l : list atom) : atom :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: rest => Nat.max x (max_atom rest)
  end.

Lemma In_max_atom : forall l x, In x l -> x <= max_atom l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| a rest IH]; intros x H.
  - inversion H.
  - simpl. destruct H as [-> | H].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; eauto | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall l : list atom, exists x, ~ In x l.
Proof.

  intro H. apply In_max_atom in H.
  exact (Nat.nle_succ_diag_l (max_atom l) H).
Qed.
```

```
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app a b => fvars a ++ fvars b

  | tm_if a b c => fvars a ++ fvars b ++ fvars c
```

OK. Let me now write the R/V Fixpoint and lemmas.

```coq
(** ** The logical relation *)

Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool =>
      locally_closed t /\
      strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall v, multi step t v -> value v -> (v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false))
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      locally_closed t /\
      strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall v, multi step t v -> value v ->
         exists T0 body,
           v = tm_abs T0 body /\
           locally_closed (tm_abs T0 body) /\
           (forall w, V T1 w -> R T2 (open body w)))
  end
with V (T : ty) (t : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool =>
      t = tm_true \/ t = tm_false
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      exists T0 body,
        t = tm_abs T0 body /\
        locally_closed (tm_abs T0 body) /\
        (forall w, V T1 w -> R T2 (open body w))
  end.
```

Potential problem: Coq might reject the mutual fixpoint because R's result in the arrow case mentions V T1 and R T2, while V's result mentions V T1 and R T2 — all on structural subterms. Should be fine.

Hmm, another potential problem: the mutual Fixpoint with `{struct T}` — Coq requires the struct argument to be the same position? Both have T as the first argument of type ty. Fine.

```
  intros T1 T2 t1 HR1 t2 HR2.
  apply R_app_aux.
  - apply R_lc with (T := Ty_Arrow T1 T2); auto.
  - apply R_cond with (T := Ty_Arrow T1 T2); auto.
  - apply R_sn with (T := Ty_Arrow T1 T2); auto.
  - apply R_lc with (T := T1); auto.
  - apply R_cond with (T := T1); auto.
  - apply R_sn with (T := T1); auto.
Qed.
```

Now R_if:
```
Lemma R_if : forall T t1 t2 t3,
  R Ty_Bool t1 -> R T t2 -> R T t3 -> R T (tm_if t1 t2 t3).
Proof.
  intros T t1 t2 t3 HR1 HR2 HR3.
  pose proof (R_lc _ _ HR1) as Hlc1.
  pose proof (R_sn _ _ HR1) as Hsn1.
  pose proof (R_cond _ _ HR1) as Hcond1.
  revert Hlc1 Hcond1.
  induction Hsn1 as [t1 Hstep1 IH1].
  intros Hlc1 Hcond1.
  assert (Hsucc : forall s, tm_if t1 t2 t3 --> s -> R T s).
  { intros s Hst. inversion Hst; subst.
    - (* ST_IfTrue *) exact HR2.  (* maybe with adjustments *)
    - (* ST_IfFalse *) exact HR3.
    - (* ST_If *) apply IH1 with (t1' := t1'); auto.
      + apply lc_step with (t := t1); auto.
      + intros v Hm Hval. apply Hcond1. exact (multi_step t1 t1' v Hstep Hm).
  }
  apply R_intro.
  - apply lc_if; [apply R_lc with (T:=Ty_Bool) | apply R_lc with (T:=T) | apply R_lc with (T:=T)]; auto.
  - apply SN_intro; intros s Hs; apply R_sn with (T := T); apply Hsucc; auto.
  - intros v Hm Hval. destruct Hm as [| s z Hs Hm].
    + inversion Hval.
    + apply (R_cond T s (Hsucc s Hs) v Hm Hval).
Qed.
```
Wait, there's an issue with `revert Hlc1 Hcond1` and then `induction Hsn1`: HR2 and HR3 are in the context and don't mention t1, fine. The motive P t := lc t -> cond_bool t -> R T (if t t2 t3). ✓ But Hsn1 was obtained by `pose proof (R_sn _ _ HR1)`, and HR1 also mentions t1! HR1 is in the context when we do induction on Hsn1. Does induction on Hsn1 generalize t1 in HR1? Coq's induction will try to abstract t1 everywhere in the goal (not in the context) — actually, `induction Hsn1` with Hsn1 : SN t1 where t1 is a local variable that appears in other hypotheses (HR1) causes an error like "Abstracting over the term t1 leads to a term which is ill-typed" or it just fails? I recall Coq reverts the goal over the variable; hypotheses in the context that mention t1 are NOT automatically reverted in newer Coq versions; instead, Coq errors if the goal's abstraction is invalid, but context hypotheses mentioning t1 are fine (they just stay, with t1 being a fixed variable, and the induction is done on Hsn1 which is a proof about that fixed t1). Hmm, wait: if t1 is a *local variable* (not a term), then induction on Hsn1 will "generalize" t1? Let me recall the exact behavior: `induction H` where H : P x with x a variable — Coq will revert x from the goal and from H, then do the induction, then reintroduce. If other hypotheses mention x, Coq needs to revert them too, and it does so automatically ("generalizing" dependencies), or it complains? I believe Coq's `induction` uses `generalize dependent` on the variables appearing in the inductive's indices — it reverts the goal and any hypotheses that depend on x... Actually, the standard behavior: `induction H` where H : P x and there are other hypotheses depending on x will error with "Cannot generalize" in some cases? Let me recall: in Coq, `induction n` where n : nat and other hypotheses mention n — it works fine; Coq clears/reverts them? Hmm, for `induction n` with n a variable, Coq does `revert n` on the goal only, and the other hypotheses mentioning n... I believe Coq reverts all hypotheses that depend on n as well (since it needs to abstract n everywhere). Yes — Coq's induction will revert dependent hypotheses in the context. But then they'd be reintroduced in each case with the new value, which is what we want for HR1? Hmm, if HR1 is reverted and reintroduced, then in the induction step we'd have HR1 about the *new* t1 — but we can't produce that! That would break the proof (we'd need to prove the goal for an arbitrary t1 given an arbitrary HR1, which is actually fine? Hmm, actually the cleanest is to formulate the lemma with all hypotheses as explicit universally quantified statements and do the induction on the SN proof after reverting everything that mentions the term. Let me restructure to avoid problems:

```
Lemma R_if : forall T t1,
  locally_closed t1 -> strongly_normalizing t1 ->
  (forall v, multi step t1 v -> value v -> V Ty_Bool v) ->
  forall t2 t3, R T t2 -> R T t3 -> R T (tm_if t1 t2 t3).

  intros T t1 Hlc1 Hsn1 Hcond1.
  revert Hlc1 Hcond1.
  induction Hsn1 as [t1 Hstep1 IH1].
  intros Hlc1 Hcond1 t2 t3 HR2 HR3.
  ...
```
This is clean: at the induction, only Hsn1 and the goal mention t1 (after revert). ✓

```
Lemma has_type_lc : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> locally_closed t.
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T H. induction H as
    [Gamma x T Heq
    | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody IHbody
    | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2
    | Gamma | Gamma
    | Gamma t1 t2 t3 T H1 IH1 H2 IH2 H3 IH3].
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_abs. destruct (fresh_atom L) as [x Hx]. apply lc_open_inv with (u := tm_fvar x) (k := 0).
    + apply lc_fvar.
    + apply IHbody; auto.
  - apply lc_app; auto.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
  - apply lc_if; auto.
Qed.
```
Note: lc_open_inv : forall t k u, lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t) -> lc_at (S k) t. With k := 0: gives lc_at 1 t1 from lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 (tm_fvar x) t1) = lc_at 0 (open t1 (tm_fvar x)). And the goal is locally_closed (tm_abs T1 t1) = lc_at 0 (tm_abs T1 t1); apply lc_abs leaves lc_at 1 t1. ✓

Hmm, `apply lc_open_inv with (u := tm_fvar x) (k := 0)` — the lemma's statement is forall t k u, ...; applying to goal lc_at 1 t1 should unify t := t1, k := 0 (since S k = 1), u := tm_fvar x. Then subgoals: lc_at 0 (tm_fvar x) and lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 (tm_fvar x) t1). ✓

So yes: IHbody x Hx : locally_closed (open t1 (tm_fvar x)) = lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 (tm_fvar x) t1). ✓

Now, the final theorem:
```
Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  pose proof (fundamental empty t T Hty (fun x => tm_fvar x)) as HR.
  - intros x. apply lc_fvar.
  - intros x U H. unfold empty in H. discriminate.
  - rewrite subst_id in HR.
    apply R_sn with (T := T). exact HR.
Qed.
```
Hmm, `pose proof (...) as HR` with remaining subgoals: the `-` bullets apply to the side conditions of the pose proof? `pose proof (f a b c) as H` will generate subgoals for the remaining arguments if they can't be inferred... Actually `pose proof (fundamental empty t T Hty (fun x => tm_fvar x))` — the term `fundamental empty t T Hty sigma` has type `(forall x, lc (sigma x)) -> env_ok empty sigma -> R T (subst sigma t)`, which is a function; pose proof will try to prove it as a whole, generating a goal of function type — not what we want. Better:

```
  assert (HR : R T (subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t)).
  { apply fundamental with (Gamma := empty); [intros x; apply lc_fvar | intros x U H; unfold empty in H; discriminate]. }
  rewrite subst_id in HR.
  apply R_sn with (T := T). exact HR.
```
Wait, `apply fundamental with (Gamma := empty)` — the goal R T (subst id t) matches the conclusion R T (subst sigma t) with sigma := id. ✓ Then the two side goals. Or simply `apply fundamental; auto` with sigma inferred:
```
  assert (HR : R T (subst (fun x : atom => tm_fvar x) t)).
  { apply fundamental.
    - intros x. apply lc_fvar.
```

```coq
(** ** Auxiliary lemmas and definitions for the normalization proof *)

(** Substitution for free variables. *)

Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)
  end.

Definition subst_update (sigma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (u : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y.

Lemma subst_update_eq :
  forall (sigma : atom -> tm) x u,
    subst_update sigma x u x = u.
Proof.
  intros. unfold subst_update. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity.
Qed.

Lemma subst_update_neq :
  forall (sigma : atom -> tm) x u y,
    y <> x -> subst_update sigma x u y = sigma y.
Proof.
  intros. unfold subst_update.
  destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
  - apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. congruence.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.

Lemma subst_open_rec :
  forall (sigma : atom -> tm) k u t,
    subst sigma (open_rec k u t) = open_rec k (subst sigma u) (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  intros sigma k u t. revert k.
  induction t; intros k; simpl.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k n); reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt1, IHt2. reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt. reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt1, IHt2, IHt3. reflexivity.
Qed.

Lemma subst_id : forall t, subst (fun x : atom => tm_fvar x) t = t.
Proof.
  induction t; simpl.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt1, IHt2. reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt. reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHt1, IHt2, IHt3. reflexivity.
Qed.

Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvars t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2 ++ fvars t3
  end.

Lemma subst_ext :
  forall t (sigma1 sigma2 : atom -> tm),
    (forall x, In x (fvars t) -> sigma1 x = sigma2 x) ->
    subst sigma1 t = subst sigma2 t.
Proof.
  induction t; intros sigma1 sigma2 Hagree; simpl.
  - reflexivity.
  - apply Hagree. simpl. auto.
  - f_equal.
    + apply IHt1. intros x Hx. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. left. exact Hx.
    + apply IHt2. intros x Hx. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. right. exact Hx.
  - f_equal. apply IHt. intros x Hx. apply Hagree. simpl. exact Hx.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - repeat f_equal.
    + apply IHt1. intros x Hx. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. left. exact Hx.
    + apply IHt2. intros x Hx. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. right. apply in_or_app. left. exact Hx.
    + apply IHt3. intros x Hx. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. right. apply in_or_app. right. exact Hx.
Qed.

Fixpoint max_atom (l : list atom) : atom :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: rest => Nat.max x (max_atom rest)
  end.

Lemma In_max_atom : forall (l : list atom) x, In x l -> x <= max_atom l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| a rest IH]; intros x H.
  - inversion H.
  - simpl. destruct H as [-> | H].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; eauto | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall l : list atom, exists x, ~ In x l.
Proof.
  intros l. exists (S (max_atom l)).
  intro H. apply In_max_atom in H.
  exact (Nat.nle_succ_diag_l (max_atom l) H).
Qed.
```

```coq
Lemma value_no_step : forall v t, value v -> ~ (v --> t).
Proof.
  intros v t Hv Hstep. destruct Hv; inversion Hstep.
Qed.

Lemma value_lc : forall v, value v -> locally_closed v.
Proof. intros v H. destruct H; auto using lc_true, lc_false. Qed.

Lemma value_SN : forall v, value v -> strongly_normalizing v.
Proof.
  intros v Hv. apply SN_intro. intros v' Hstep.
  exact (value_no_step v v' Hv Hstep).
Qed.

Lemma multi_of_value : forall v v', value v -> multi step v v' -> v' = v.
Proof.
  intros v v' Hv Hm. destruct Hm as [| s z Hs Hm].
  - reflexivity.
  - exact (False_ind _ (value_no_step v s Hv Hs)).
Qed.

Lemma SN_step : forall t t', strongly_normalizing t -> t --> t' -> strongly_normalizing t'.
Proof.
  intros t t' Hsn Hstep. destruct Hsn as [t0 Hprem]. exact (Hprem t' Hstep).
Qed.
```

```coq
(** ** Substitution of free variables *)

Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)
  end.

Definition subst_update (sigma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (u : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y.

Lemma subst_update_eq : forall (sigma : atom -> tm) x u,
  subst_update sigma x u x = u.
Proof. intros; unfold subst_update; rewrite Nat.eqb_refl; reflexivity. Qed.

Lemma subst_update_neq : forall (sigma : atom -> tm) x u y,
  y <> x -> subst_update sigma x u y = sigma y.
Proof.
  intros; unfold subst_update.
  destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
  - apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. congruence.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.

Lemma subst_open_rec : forall (sigma : atom -> tm) k u t,
  subst sigma (open_rec k u t) = open_rec k (subst sigma u) (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  intros sigma k u t; revert k.
  induction t as [i | x | a IHa b IHb | Ty b IHb | | | a IHa b IHb c IHBc];
  intros k; simpl.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k i); reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHa, IHb; reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHb; reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHa, IHb, IHBc; reflexivity.
Qed.
```
Careful in the abs case: goal after simpl is `tm_abs Ty (subst sigma (open_rec (S k) u b)) = tm_abs Ty (open_rec (S k) (subst sigma u) (subst sigma b))`. `rewrite IHb` where IHb : forall k, subst sigma (open_rec k u b) = open_rec k (subst sigma u) (subst sigma b) — with k := S k. `rewrite IHb` should instantiate. ✓

```coq
Lemma subst_id : forall t, subst (fun x : atom => tm_fvar x) t = t.
Proof.
  induction t as [i | x | a IHa b IHb | Ty b IHb | | | a IHa b IHb c IHBc]; simpl.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHa, IHb; reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHb; reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHa, IHb, IHBc; reflexivity.
Qed.

Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvars t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2 ++ fvars t3
  end.

Lemma subst_ext : forall t (sigma1 sigma2 : atom -> tm),
  (forall x, In x (fvars t) -> sigma1 x = sigma2 x) ->
  subst sigma1 t = subst sigma2 t.
Proof.
  induction t as [i | x | a IHa b IHb | Ty b IHb | | | a IHa b IHb c IHBc];
  intros sigma1 sigma2 Hagree; simpl.
  - reflexivity.
  - apply Hagree; simpl; auto.
  - f_equal.
    + apply IHa. intros y Hy. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. left. exact Hy.
    + apply IHb. intros y Hy. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. right. exact Hy.
  - f_equal. apply IHb. intros y Hy. apply Hagree. simpl. exact Hy.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - repeat f_equal.
    + apply IHa. intros y Hy. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. left. exact Hy.
    + apply IHb. intros y Hy. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. right. apply in_or_app. left. exact Hy.
    + apply IHBc. intros y Hy. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. right. apply in_or_app. right. exact Hy.
Qed.
```

For R_if_aux, the statement:
```
Lemma R_if_aux : forall T (t1 : tm),
  locally_closed t1 ->
  (forall v, multi step t1 v -> value v -> V Ty_Bool v) ->
  strongly_normalizing t1 ->
  forall t2 t3, R T t2 -> R T t3 -> R T (tm_if t1 t2 t3).
```

Proof:
```
  intros T t1 Hlc1 Hcond1 Hsn1.
  revert Hlc1 Hcond1.
  induction Hsn1 as [t1 Hstep1 IH1].
  intros Hlc1 Hcond1 t2 t3 HR2 HR3.
  assert (Hsucc : forall s, tm_if t1 t2 t3 --> s -> R T s).
  { intros s Hst. inversion Hst; subst.
    - (* ST_IfTrue *) exact HR2.
    - (* ST_IfFalse *) exact HR3.
    - (* ST_If *) apply (IH1 t1' H).  (* names *)
      + apply lc_step with (t := t1); auto.
      + intros w Hm Hval. apply Hcond1. exact (multi_step t1 t1' w H Hm).
      + exact HR2.
      + exact HR3. }
  apply R_intro.
  - apply lc_if; [exact Hlc1 | apply R_lc; auto | apply R_lc; auto].
  - apply SN_intro; intros s Hs; apply R_sn with (T := T); apply Hsucc; auto.
  - intros v Hm Hval. destruct Hm as [| s z Hs Hm].
    + inversion Hval.
    + apply (R_cond T s (Hsucc s Hs) v Hm Hval).
Qed.
```
Hmm, the ST_If case's goal: R T (tm_if t1' t2 t3) where t2, t3 are our fixed branches — but after inversion of `tm_if t1 t2 t3 --> s`, the constructor ST_If unifies its `t1 t2 t3` with ours: so the goal's t2/t3 are ours. ✓ Then IH1 t1' H gives: lc t1' -> cond t1' -> forall t2 t3, R T t2 -> R T t3 -> R T (tm_if t1' t2 t3). Lemma lc_step : forall t t', t --> t' -> locally_closed t -> locally_closed t'.
```coq
Proof.
  intros t t' Hstep.

    [T0 body v Hlcabs Hval
    | t1 t1' t2 Hs1 IHs1 Hlc2

  - (* ST_IfFalse *) apply lc_if_inv in Hlc as [H1 [H2 H3]]. exact H2.
```

Now subst_lc_at:
```coq
Lemma subst_lc_at : forall t k (sigma : atom -> tm),
  lc_at k t -> (forall x, lc_at k (sigma x)) -> lc_at k (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  induction t as [i | x | a IHa b IHb | Ty b IHb | | | a IHa b IHb c IHBc];
  intros k sigma Ht Hs; simpl.
  - exact Ht.
  - apply Hs.
  - apply lc_app.
    + apply IHa with (k := k) (sigma := sigma). apply lc_app_inv in Ht as [H1 H2]. exact H1. exact Hs.
    + apply IHb with (k := k) (sigma := sigma). apply lc_app_inv in Ht as [H1 H2]. exact H2. exact Hs.
  - apply lc_abs. apply IHb with (k := S k) (sigma := sigma).
```

```coq
(** ** Substitution of free variables *)

Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)
  end.

Definition subst_update (sigma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (u : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y.

Lemma subst_update_eq : forall (sigma : atom -> tm) x u,
  subst_update sigma x u x = u.
Proof. intros. unfold subst_update. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity. Qed.

Lemma subst_update_neq : forall (sigma : atom -> tm) x u y,
  y <> x -> subst_update sigma x u y = sigma y.
Proof.
  intros. unfold subst_update.
  destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
  - apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. congruence.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.

Lemma subst_open_rec : forall (sigma : atom -> tm) k u t,
  subst sigma (open_rec k u t) = open_rec k (subst sigma u) (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  intros sigma k u t. revert k.
  induction t as [i | x | a IHa b IHb | Ty b IHb | | | a IHa b IHb c IHBc];
    intros k; simpl.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k i); reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHa, IHb. reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHb. reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHa, IHb, IHBc. reflexivity.
Qed.

Lemma subst_id : forall t, subst (fun x : atom => tm_fvar x) t = t.
Proof.
  induction t as [i | x | a IHa b IHb | Ty b IHb | | | a IHa b IHb c IHBc]; simpl.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHa, IHb. reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHb. reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IHa, IHb, IHBc. reflexivity.
Qed.

Fixpoint fvars (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvars t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fvars t1 ++ fvars t2 ++ fvars t3
  end.

Lemma subst_ext : forall t (sigma1 sigma2 : atom -> tm),
  (forall x, In x (fvars t) -> sigma1 x = sigma2 x) ->
  subst sigma1 t = subst sigma2 t.
Proof.
  induction t as [i | x | a IHa b IHb | Ty b IHb | | | a IHa b IHb c IHBc];
    intros sigma1 sigma2 Hagree; simpl.
  - reflexivity.
  - apply Hagree. simpl. auto.
  - f_equal.
    + apply IHa. intros y Hy. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. left. exact Hy.
    + apply IHb. intros y Hy. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. right. exact Hy.
  - f_equal. apply IHb. intros y Hy. apply Hagree. simpl. exact Hy.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - f_equal.
    + apply IHa. intros y Hy. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. left. exact Hy.
    + apply IHb. intros y Hy. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. right. apply in_or_app. left. exact Hy.
    + apply IHBc. intros y Hy. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. right. apply in_or_app. right. exact Hy.
Qed.
```

```coq
Lemma R_forward : forall T t t', R T t -> t --> t' -> R T t'.
Proof.
  intros T t t' HR Hstep.
  apply R_intro.
  - apply lc_step with (t := t); [exact Hstep | apply R_lc with (T := T); exact HR].
  - apply SN_step with (t := t); [apply R_sn with (T := T); exact HR | exact Hstep].
  - intros v Hm Hval. apply (R_cond T t HR v).
    + exact (multi_step t t' v Hstep Hm).
    + exact Hval.
Qed.
```

```coq
Definition env_ok (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> R T (sigma x).

Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma,
    (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) ->
    env_ok Gamma sigma ->
    R T (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty.
  induction Hty as
    [Gamma x T0 Heq
    | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody IHbody
    | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2
    | Gamma
    | Gamma
    | Gamma t1 t2 t3 T0 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 H3 IH3].
  - (* T_Var *) intros sigma HlcS Henv. simpl. apply Henv. exact Heq.
  - (* T_Abs *) intros sigma HlcS Henv. simpl.
    assert (Hlc_abs : locally_closed (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1))).
    { apply lc_abs. apply subst_lc_at with (k := 1).
      - apply lc_abs_inv with (T := T1).
        apply has_type_lc with (Gamma := Gamma) (T := Ty_Arrow T1 T2).
        exact (T_Abs L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody).
      - intros x. apply lc_mono with (k := 0); [apply HlcS | lia]. }
    apply R_intro.
    + exact Hlc_abs.
    + apply value_SN. apply v_abs. exact Hlc_abs.
    + intros v Hm Hval. destruct Hm as [| s Hs Hm].
      * simpl. exists T1, (subst sigma t1). split; [reflexivity | split; [exact Hlc_abs | ]].
        intros w HVw.
        destruct (fresh_atom (L ++ fvars t1)) as [x Hx].
        assert (HxL : ~ In x L) by (intro H; apply Hx; apply in_or_app; left; exact H).
        assert (Hxfv : ~ In x (fvars t1)) by (intro H; apply Hx; apply in_or_app; right; exact H).
        assert (HRx : R T2 (subst (subst_update sigma x w) (open t1 (tm_fvar x)))).
        { apply (IHbody x HxL (subst_update sigma x w)).
          - intros y. unfold subst_update. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:Ey.
            + apply Nat.eqb_eq in Ey. subst y. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. apply V_lc. exact HVw.
            + apply HlcS.
          - intros y U Hy. unfold subst_update. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:Ey.
            + apply Nat.eqb_eq in Ey. subst y.
              unfold update in Hy. simpl in Hy. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl in Hy.
              injection Hy as HU. subst U. rewrite subst_update_eq. apply V_R. exact HVw.
            + unfold update in Hy. rewrite Ey in Hy. apply Henv. exact Hy. }
        simpl in HRx.
        rewrite subst_open_rec in HRx. ...
```

Wait, that means destruct Hm generalized the source `tm_app t1 t2` into a variable x in the refl case. That's odd but it's how `destruct` on an inductive with indices works when the index is not a variable: it abstracts the index into a new variable and adds equations... but here apparently the equation was consumed by substituting? The issue: now the goal V T2 x has x as a *variable*, so I cannot prove it — indeed, V T2 x is not provable for arbitrary x. There must be an equation x = tm_app t1 t2 somewhere... but the context shows none. Hmm, maybe destruct cleared it via "dependent destruction"-like behavior.

The reason: `destruct Hm as [| s z Hs Hm]` where the index term is compound makes Coq use "dependent elimination" which generalizes the index (introducing x) and creates equations linking x to the original term. But `destruct` cannot keep the equation for the source because it's not a variable... In this case, it replaced the source with x everywhere including in Hsucc, losing the link. That's bad.

Standard fix: use `remember` to make the source a variable first, then destruct, keeping the equation:
```
remember (tm_app t1 t2) as st eqn:Est.
destruct Hm as [| s z Hs Hm].
- (* refl *) subst st. inversion Hval.
```
Hmm, with remember, the goal becomes about st, and the equation Est : st = tm_app t1 t2 stays. Then in the refl case, st = tm_app t1 t2 and goal V T2 st; rewrite Est; then value (tm_app t1 t2) — inversion.

Alternatively, use `inversion Hm` rather than destruct: inversion on multi with the source index compound might also generalize. Hmm.

```
Lemma multi_value_step : forall t v, multi step t v -> value v ->
```

Lemma multi_cases : forall a b, multi step a b -> (a = b) \/ (exists c, step a c /\ multi step c b).
Proof. intros a b H. destruct H as [x | x y z Hxy Hyz]. - left; reflexivity. - right; exists y; auto. Qed.

```
intros v Hm Hval. destruct (multi_cases _ _ Hm) as [Heq | (s & Hs & Hm')].
```

Let me prove multi_cases:
```
Lemma multi_cases : forall (a b : tm), multi step a b ->
  a = b \/ (exists c, step a c /\ multi step c b).
Proof.
  intros a b H. destruct H as [x | x y z Hxy Hyz].
  - left; reflexivity.
  - right. exists y. split; assumption.
Qed.
```
Hmm, `destruct H` where H : multi step a b with a, b variables — in the multi_refl case, a and b get unified (destruct may substitute one), but the goal `a = b ∨ ...` should be solved by reflexivity either way; in the multi_step case, x := a, z := b, and we have Hxy : step a y, Hyz : multi step y b. ✓ This should work since a, b are variables (destruct can substitute). Let me look at the R/V definition and check the intended semantics once more:

```
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => lc t ∧ SN t ∧ (∀v, t -->* v → value v → v = true ∨ v = false)
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => lc t ∧ SN t ∧ (∀v, t -->* v → value v → ∃T0 body, v = abs T0 body ∧ lc(abs T0 body) ∧ ∀w, V T1 w → R T2 (open body w))
  end
with V (T : ty) (t : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => t = true ∨ t = false
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => ∃T0 body, t = abs T0 body ∧ lc(abs T0 body) ∧ ∀w, V T1 w → R T2 (open body w)
  end.
```

Good. R is the reducibility predicate on all terms (lc + SN + all reachable values in V); V is the value relation.

Key lemmas:
- R_lc, R_sn, R_cond, R_intro ✓
- V_lc, V_value, V_sn, V_R ✓
- R_forward (closure under one step) ✓
- R_app (application lemma), R_if (conditional lemma) ✓
- has_type_lc, subst_lc_at, lc_step ✓
- fundamental theorem with env_ok ✓
- normalization ✓

One thing to double check: the proof of `R_app_aux` uses `Hcond1` for the arrow value at `multi_refl`; it requires `value (tm_abs T t)` produced via `v_abs` from the lc premise given by inversion of ST_AppAbs. ✓.
